Waiting for the Linux machine and the Windows PC: the shared-file runs and the reference regeneration (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

Phase 19 **Day 253 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the SQLite backend on `:memory:`.** `database::SqliteDatabase` opens private in-memory databases through a minimal engine VFS (engine mutexes via `SQLITE_CONFIG_MUTEX`, no files yet), with statements, transactions, savepoints, schema version and an error-code mapping; the conformance suite runs on it: 30 of 32 cases pass, 2 skip (they need two connections to one database; Day 254). Findings: SQLite loses the sign of `-0.0` and cannot store a NaN (documented, NaN bind is `Misuse`). Runs: plain `ran 2288 of 5979 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1756 of 5979`, tsan `ran 1438 of 5979` (known failures only); Clang non-unity and GCC clean; docs 7 of 7 (also fixed a doc link in `cstr/number.h` that broke `docgen` on Linux). Code review: 3 findings, all fixed. Open: the Windows PC build. **Next: Day 254** (SQLite files, locks, crash safety).

Phase 19 **Day 254 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): SQLite on files.** The engine's SQLite VFS (`sqlite_vfs.cpp`) reaches the disk only through `io::File`/`io::Path`, keeps SQLite's five lock levels and the write-ahead-log index in process memory (version-2 VFS), and `SqliteDatabase::Open` opens files (WAL by default, `Delete`/`Truncate` selectable, `NotFound`, `Corrupt`, read-only). New `os::WallClockUnixMillis` and `os::FillRandomBytes`. The conformance suite passes 32 of 32 on a file; a fault-injection sweep (the Nth write, sync or truncation fails, or a crash stops all file operations) leaves the old or the new rows and a passing integrity check in all three journal modes. **Not built: exclusion of a second process** (the file layer has no file locks; KNOWN-ISSUES). Runs: plain `ran 2331 of 6104 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1797 of 6104`, tsan `ran 1479 of 6104` (known failures only); Clang non-unity and GCC clean; docs 7 of 7. Code review: 1 finding, fixed. Open: the Windows PC build (including `bcrypt` for `foundation_base`) and macOS `os/` functions. **Next: Day 255** (settings, statement cache, concurrency, benchmark).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 337 (2026-11-11, the Mac): the `os` backend; the foundation's base tests run on macOS.** `os/linux/os.cpp` is split: the calls Linux and macOS share are in the new `os/posix/os_posix.cpp`, and `os/macos/os.cpp` adds macOS's clock (`CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW`, which stops in sleep like Linux's), `getentropy`, `task_info` and `proc_pidinfo` process figures, and a `LoadLibrary` that never searches the current directory (macOS's `dlopen` would). `os/macos/session.mm` reads the window server's session dictionary (it answered `locked`: the Mac was locked). The build found `Hash<usize>` missing on macOS (`usize` and `u64` are different types there), fixed in `containers/hash_map.h`; `module_deps_check` now scans `.mm` files. `tests/foundation` (base tests) and `tests/foundation_sanitized` join the macOS build, with the new `tests/macos/os_macos_test.cpp` (three flavors; a child process checks non-ASCII arguments and a UTF-8 log line through a pipe). Runs (plain ctest; the test script comes on Day 338): `build-macos` 379 of 379, `-L phase0` 99, `phase10` 110, `phase16` 114, `phase21` 37, all passed; non-unity 379 of 379; the five gates clean by hand. Code review: 9 findings, 6 fixed (one real bug, with a test shown to fail first: `WriteAllWithTimeout` wrote nothing to `/dev/null`, whose `poll` gives `POLLNVAL` on macOS), 1 in part (the reference waits for Linux), 2 declined with reasons. Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared files, the session check over ssh and a look at Terminal.app (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 338 (the test tooling and gates on macOS).

Phase 19 **Day 255 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the SQLite backend is complete.** `database.journal_mode`, `.synchronous`, `.busy_timeout_ms`, `.integrity_check` and `.statement_cache` settings (`RegisterDatabaseSettings`, `OptionsFromSettings`); a per-connection statement cache (hits skip compiling; point reads about 2x faster); an integrity check at open; a debug check against two threads in one connection; concurrency tests on threads (one writer with readers, queued writers, `Busy` for a second writer, savepoints) in plain, asan and tsan. tsan needs a suppression for SQLite's lock-free WAL index header (`tests/database/tsan.supp`, two functions). Runs: plain `ran 2340 of 6131 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1807 of 6134`, tsan `ran 1489 of 6134` (known failures only); Clang non-unity and GCC clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: inline, no findings. Open: the Windows PC build. **Next: Day 256** (storage: component registry).

Phase 19 **Day 256 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the component registry and the canonical encoding.** New `foundation/bytes` (`ByteWriter`/`ByteReader`: little-endian, bit-exact floats, shortest-form varints, bounds-checked reads; shared with Phase 20) and the module `engine/storage` (layer 11): `ComponentRegistry` with stable ids, names, versions and fields by pointer to member (`Field<&T::m>("m")`: integers, floats, bool, vectors, quaternion, matrix, box, enums with a declared range, strings, arrays, entity references; any other member type does not compile), `Encode`/`Decode` of one component, the manifest check, `UnregisteredWithData(world)` and the builtin components. Golden bytes and every-cut truncation tests. Runs: plain `ran 2363 of 6180 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1819 of 6180`, tsan `ran 1501 of 6180` (known failures only); Clang non-unity and GCC clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 3 findings, all fixed. Open: the Windows PC build. **Next: Day 257** (versions, upgrades, hostile-input tests).
Phase 19 **Day 256 follow-up:** `ENGINE_TRY` early-return macro (`lang/status_try.h`) and `[[nodiscard]]` on `database::Status`, `storage::Status` and `bytes::ByteError`; nine ignored results fixed. Runs as Day 256 (`ran 2363 of 6180` plain, `1819` asan, `1501` tsan).

Phase 19 **Day 257 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): versions, upgrades and hostile input.** `ComponentDesc` upgrade chains (one step per version, checked at registration), `DecodeRecord`, `TooOld`/`TooNew`, custom codecs, `UnknownPolicy` with `Resolve` and `OpaqueComponent` (an older program keeps a newer program's bytes), record framing, storage limits as settings, and a fuzz harness (21 kinds x 100,000 mutated encodings, canonical re-encode property; a planted bug fails it). Runs: plain `ran 2363 of 6180 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1819 of 6180`, tsan `ran 1501 of 6180` (known failures only); Clang non-unity and GCC clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 1 finding, fixed. Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-257. **Next: Day 258** (stable identities).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 338 (2026-11-11, the Mac): the test tooling and the gates on macOS; side phases.** `scripts/test-phase.sh` runs under the Mac's `/bin/bash` 3.2 (default `build-macos`; `sysctl` for the CPU count). The new `SIDE_PHASES` file (26) makes `--affected` add a side phase the build has tests of, marked in the summary (`phases: 19 26 (side)`), and `test_phase_labels` fails on a map phase above `CURRENT_PHASE` that is not a side phase, or a stale side-phase entry. All twelve source gates run on macOS. `platform_neutral_check` rule 7 keeps Apple's headers and `.m`/`.mm` files in the macOS directories. Every include-reading gate now counts `#import` (found today: a `.mm` could have reached `<vector>` or an upper layer unseen). **Plan amended:** docgen needs the whole foundation, so the reference and `docs_check`/`links`/`lint` move to Day 345 and `docs_samples` to Day 294. Runs (macOS, the script): `ran 498 of 498 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; ...)` in unity and non-unity; `--affected` plain 344, asan 77, tsan 77 of 498 (everything: CMake changed); scoped to a plan change `ran 29 of 498 tests (phases: 19 26 (side); flavors: plain; ...)`, asan 11, tsan 11; all passed. Code review: 10 findings, all fixed, with planted cases. **macOS commands:** `cmake --preset macos-clang`, then `/bin/bash scripts/test-phase.sh --affected [--flavor all]`. Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared script and gate changes (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 339 (the file layer).

Phase 19 **Day 258 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): stable identities.** `EntityKey` (ordering, hash, text form), `KeyGenerator` (random or seeded), `EntityKeyComponent` and `KeyIndex` (kept in step by the change stamps and removal log; duplicates never both indexed), references resolved with `MissingReference` or a counted null, and `ecs::Parent` stored as `ParentLink` (`CaptureParents` / `ApplyParents`, linear, cycle and depth checks). Runs: plain `ran 2403 of 6300 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1859 of 6300`, tsan `ran 1541 of 6300` (known failures only); GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 4 findings, all fixed. Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-258. **Next: Day 259** (the store interface).

Phase 19 **Day 259 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the record store.** `storage::Store` (put, get, remove, scan in key order, count, batches, metadata; one writer, readers on any thread), `EntityRecord` with a content hash, `MemoryStore` (the reference, with injectable failure), the options and results (`Selection`, `SaveOptions`, `LoadOptions`, `Report`, `CancelToken`), `WorldStorage` declared (`Delete` and `List` work; `Save` and `Load` are Day 260) and the store conformance suite that the SQL store joins on Days 262-263. Runs: plain `ran 2425 of 6344 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1870 of 6344`, tsan `ran 1552 of 6344`; GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: no code defect; 2 slips in the day's own tests and docs fixed. Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-259. **Next: Day 260** (full save and load).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 339 (2026-11-11, the Mac): the file layer on macOS, part I.** The Linux file backend moved to `engine/foundation/io/posix/`, shared with macOS. Each system adds two hooks (file times; durable sync) and its own executable path and standard directories (`io/linux/`, `io/macos/special_folders.cpp`). On macOS `File::Sync` and `SyncDirectory` use `F_FULLFSYNC`. Measured on this SSD: 18.2 ms against `fsync`'s 0.39 ms for 4 KiB; a 4 KiB `WriteFileAtomic` takes 39 ms. The errno table gains Darwin's codes (`EOPNOTSUPP` is its own number there). The temporary volume is case- and normalization-insensitive, which the tests check without the layer folding anything. `tests/io` runs on macOS in three flavors (the async tests wait for jobs, Day 341). Runs (macOS, the script): `--affected` plain `ran 405 of 657 tests (phases: all; ...)`, asan 126, tsan 126, all passed; non-unity `ran 657 of 657`. Code review: 10 findings, 8 fixed (a FIFO test that could hang or kill the run; the `F_FULLFSYNC` fallback missing `EOPNOTSUPP`), 1 in part (the reference waits for Linux), 1 declined (`F_BARRIERFSYNC`, unverified). Waiting: the Linux run of the moved backend, which has never compiled there (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 340 (directories, `copyfile`, standard directories, `dispatch_io`).

Phase 19 **Day 260 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): saving and loading a world.** `WorldStorage::Save` and `Load` (full): one record per keyed entity in key order, stored components, parent as a key, presence of derived components, unknown components kept opaquely; a load decodes and checks everything before it touches the world, then creates entities in key order (`Create`, `Replace`, `Merge`). New type-erased ECS calls (`CreateFromSet`, `AddDefault`, `RemoveById`, `CellOf`, `HasById`, `EnsureArchetype`). 10,000 entities in 64 archetypes round trip bit-exact and in the same order; a store failing at every write, a damaged store and a cancelled load leave things unchanged. Runs: plain `ran 2440 of 6389 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1885 of 6389`, tsan `ran 1567 of 6389` (known failures only); GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 4 findings, fixed. Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-260; four filed limits (KNOWN-ISSUES). **Next: Day 261** (incremental saves).
Phase 19 **Day 261 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): incremental saves.** `SaveSession` remembers a world's saves to a store (tick, removal clock, record hashes, keys); an incremental save visits only changed chunks, encodes their entities, writes only records whose hash changed and takes removals from the ECS removal log, falling back to a hash comparison with the store (new session, restart, lost log). The store equals a full save after 500 random frames at saving intervals 1, 2 and 7; idle worlds write nothing. Idle save at 100,000 entities 18 ms, 1 percent changed 19 ms (one run) to 192 ms (spread). Runs: plain `ran 2451 of 6422 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`; GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs 8194/8194. asan `ran 1896 of 6422`, tsan `ran 1578 of 6422` (known failures only). Review: 2 findings (1 filed, 1 recorded). Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-261. **Next: Day 262** (SQL store).
Phase 19 **Day 262 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the SQL store.** `storage::SqlStore` puts the record store on a `database::Connection` (one row per entity, key and hash as big-endian blobs, metadata table, portable SQL subset, statements prepared once), with versioned migrations at open (`ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` in place when the backend supports it, a rebuild otherwise; a real version-1 database upgrades, a newer one is `TooNew` and untouched). It passes the store conformance suite on SQLite in memory and in a file, survives a failure at every write and sync of a batch, and carries a world saved incrementally and loaded back. Runs: plain `ran 2472 of 6485 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1917 of 6485`, tsan `ran 1599 of 6485` (known failures only); GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs pass. Review: 2 findings (1 fixed, 1 declined). Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-262. **Next: Day 263** (columns and bulk queries).
Phase 19 **Day 263 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): column layout, bulk paths and queries.** Components flagged `kComponentQueryable` get a `c_<name>` table (one column per scalar or vector field) written beside the entity blob, which stays the source of truth; `SqlStore::FindKeys`/`CountWhere` answer field queries in key order, optional indexes, field additions update the table (`ALTER` or rebuild) and refill it from the blobs; `Store::PutMany` (16 rows per statement) is used by every save; schema version 3 makes `entity` `WITHOUT ROWID`. 100,000 entities: blob-only save 0.45 s, with columns 0.93 s, load 0.6 s either way, a query of 18,268 hits 6.8 ms (0.5 ms indexed). `FindKeys` equals a brute-force scan for 66 column/operator pairs over 10,000 entities. Runs: plain `ran 2480 of 6509 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1925 of 6509`, tsan `ran 1607 of 6509` (known failures only); GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs pass. Review: 3 findings (2 fixed, 1 declined). Open: the Windows PC build of Days 251-263. **Next: Day 264** (settings, async saves, demo).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 340 (2026-11-11, the Mac): the file layer on macOS, part II.** `Copy` moves its data with `fcopyfile` for a regular file and the shared loop otherwise. It is not an APFS clone, because a clone keeps the source's times. The user directories (`io/macos/special_folders.mm`) take `$HOME` first, as `$XDG_*` and `%APPDATA%` come first elsewhere, then `NSSearchPathForDirectoriesInDomains`, which ignores `$HOME` (checked), then the password database. **`dispatch_io` was measured and is not used:** 1,000 random 4 KiB reads took 13-16 ms on the default executor against 232-239 ms through `dispatch_io`, and one 64 MiB read 50-55 ms against 70-75 ms. The io-level async tests run with jobs on Day 341. Runs (macOS, the script): `--affected` plain `ran 408 of 666 tests (phases: all; ...)`, asan 129, tsan 129, all passed (slow: Spotlight was indexing). Code review: 9 findings, 7 fixed (a `Copy` regression for FIFO sources, with a test shown to fail first), 1 in part, 1 declined. Waiting: the Linux run of the shared changes (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 341 (the job system).

Phase 19 **Days 251-263 on the Windows PC (2026-11-11).** SQLite, the database interface and storage build in the four Windows trees after three fixes (MSVC C2797 on the `InitializerList` defaults of `ComponentDesc`, C4702 in `EncodeValue`, and a script test that asked for tsan, refused on Windows); plain `ran 2294 of 4455 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 249 s` (the known DX12 device flake, passed 3 of 3), asan `ran 1894 of 4455 tests (phases: all; flavors: asan; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 157 s`; the 510 phase 19 tests pass, docs 7 of 7. Review: no findings. The three fixes wait for the Linux machine and the Mac (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Day 264 is next on the Linux machine.

Phase 19 **Day 264 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): settings, asynchronous saves, autosave and the demo.** `storage.path`, `storage.layout`, `storage.autosave_seconds` are settings and `LocalStorage::OpenFromSettings` opens the storage from them; `Save` is now plan (reads the world, encodes on workers), write, adopt; `WorldStorage::SaveAsync`/`LoadAsync` with `AsyncSave`/`AsyncLoad` handles (a second writer is `Busy`, cancel leaves the store as it was, the world may change as soon as `SaveAsync` returns), `Autosaver`; `game/storage_demo` saves a city with its spatial sets, loads it into a fresh world and gets the same query answers, then autosaves a running world; tutorial `saving-a-world.md`. Async equals sync byte for byte over 104 worlds with 1-8 participants. Runs: plain `ran 2492 of 6541 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1935 of 6541`, tsan `ran 1617 of 6541` (only the known Day 235 tsan failure). Clang and GCC non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 5 findings (see the day file). Open: the Windows PC build; the render draw-list comparison (needs Phase 22). **Next: Day 265** (benchmarks, hostile files, closing run, uncapped review).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 341 (2026-11-11, the Mac): the job system on macOS.** `jobs/macos/thread_platform.cpp` covers the CPU list (every active CPU), the thread ids and the names (set from inside each worker, cut to 63 bytes on a UTF-8 boundary). **Workers are not pinned:** macOS has no affinity API, so a new `CanPinThreads()` hook keeps the pool from trying. `engine/jobs` and `tests/jobs` build in three flavors, and the io-level async tests now run too. Measured (Debug): throughput 1.00x, 1.99x, 3.79x and 6.07x for 1, 2, 4 and 8 participants; an idle worker wakes in a median 26-27 us. Found: the TSan canary aborted on macOS (`abort_on_error` defaults to 1 there), so its test now sets the Linux behavior. Reading pipes in `ReadFileAsync` was tried and reverted after review (it could block the I/O thread). Runs (macOS, the script): `--affected` plain `ran 501 of 946 tests (phases: all; ...)`, asan 222, tsan 223, all passed. Non-unity: `ran 946 of 946`, with one failure: `Throughput scales with participant count`, a timing test that is flaky under parallel load here as on Windows (3 failures in 4 parallel runs, 6 passes in 6 alone; `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Code review: 9 findings, 8 fixed, 1 moved to Day 342. Next: Day 342 (debugging: stack traces).
Phase 19 **Day 265 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): benchmarks, hostile files, docs, closing run and uncapped review; Phase 19 complete on Linux.** Benchmarks (`tests/storage/storage_bench.cpp`) and a corruption fuzz (`hostile_file_test.cpp`) added; guides and spec finished. Closing run: `ran 6556 of 6556 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: included) in 263 s`; failures only the three known X11 window tests and the known Day 235 tsan scene segfault; docs 7 of 7 (8273/8273 symbols); GCC and Clang non-unity clean. Uncapped review (~60 findings): 7 fixed (2 with regression tests), the rest filed in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`. **Open:** the Windows PC build and test of Days 264-265; one missed pass condition (random 1% incremental save of 1,000,000 entities 1.23 s against 0.25 s, options in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`) for the user. `CURRENT_PHASE` stays 19 until Phase 20's first day; Phase 20 needs day files before it starts. **Next: Phase 20 planning (day files).**

Phase 19 **Day 265, Windows half (2026-11-11, Windows PC): Phase 19 complete on both machines.** The four Windows trees build clean with nothing to fix; closing run `ran 4487 of 4487 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: included) in 330 s`; two docs failures fixed (the saving-a-world tutorial ignored `[[nodiscard]]` results, MSVC C4834; `jobs.md` stale after the Mac's Day 305 (written as Day 289 before the renumbering)), then `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7; both storage demos pass. Benchmark (Release): 1M blob save 3.85 s and load 3.24 s (met), async 4.5x with 8 participants (met); the 1-percent incremental save misses on Windows for neighbouring changes too (381-392 ms against 0.25 s; 128 ms on Linux). Review of the fix: no findings. `CURRENT_PHASE` stays 19 until Phase 20 starts. **Next: Phase 20 (networking).**

Phase 21 (side) **Day 290 (2026-11-11, the Mac; done as Day 290 before the Phase 20 insertion; Day 290 again since the Phase 21 renumbering): debugging on macOS, part I.** libbacktrace builds for Mach-O. `engine_dsym` (`ENGINE_DSYM`, on by default) writes a dSYM beside each test binary, so stack traces have function, file and line on macOS: tested in the executable and in a dylib, with a system library's frame named by its module. The module list comes from dyld's add-image and remove-image callbacks and covers each image's `__TEXT` segment (the span of all its segments overlapped other images in the shared cache). The crash context matches `sysctl`. Thread ids are the job system's. The rest of the debug backend answers "unsupported" from a temporary file until Days 291-292. Found and fixed: libbacktrace cannot read a bundle (plug-ins must be dylibs), and same-named sources in one archive lost their debug information to `dsymutil` (renamed; the configure now refuses such names on Apple). Runs (macOS, the script, crash tests included): `ran 1253 of 1253 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; ...)`, all passed in unity; non-unity the same except the known-flaky throughput test. Code review: 10 findings, all fixed (the worst: the module list could race with `dlclose`). Next: Day 291 (crash handler, death tests).

Phase 20 **Day 266 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): design spec, three spikes, phase setup.** `CURRENT_PHASE` is 20. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-11-networking-design.md` records the decisions, the spike figures and the benchmark pass conditions. Spikes (throwaway): one epoll reactor thread with four resuming threads reaches 1.0-1.09 million echo round trips per second at 1,000 loopback connections (0.74-0.76 wake-ups per round trip), so the reactor is a second non-participant thread beside `jobs::IoThread`, not an extension of it (Day 269 amended); loopback UDP shows no loss or reordering at 10k/100k/500k paced datagrams per second with a draining receiver, while a stalled receiver keeps 92 of a 20,000 burst with the default buffer and 3,640 with 4 MiB (`net.recv_buffer_bytes` defaults to 4 MiB; loss tests run on the simulated transport); loopback TCP, UDP and IPv6 work in this environment. Windows (`WSAPoll` first) is not measured. **Waiting for the user: the QUIC / TLS / encryption decision (spec section 3 (3); the recommendation is authenticated encryption on an audited primitive over the UDP transport with QUIC and TLS deferred); its sizes and per-platform facts are from documentation and memory, not measured. It is needed before Day 280, not before Day 267.** Runs: plain `ran 2501 of 6564 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1942 of 6564`, tsan `ran 1624 of 6564` (known X11 failures and one flake filed in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`); docs links fixed (the new spec is linked from `docs/README.md`). No engine code, so no day review. **Next: Day 267** (addresses, endpoints and network errors).

Phase 20 **Day 267 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): addresses, endpoints and network errors.** New module `engine/net` (layer 11): `net::Address` (IPv4/IPv6, strict parsing, RFC 5952 formatting, scopes by index or name, classification, order, hash), `Endpoint`, `EndpointSet`, `net::Status`/`ErrorCode`; guide `docs/guide/net.md`; `tests/net` (18 cases, phase 20, plain/asan/tsan). The errno/WSA mapping tests moved to Days 268 and 278. Runs: plain `ran 2519 of 6618 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1960 of 6618`, tsan `ran 1642 of 6618` (no failures in the final runs; every run was widened to all phases because the day adds a module and so changes CMakeLists.txt and tests/phases.txt); GCC non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 10 findings, 8 fixed, 1 declined, 1 plan correction (day file). Open: the Windows PC build. **Next: Day 268** (Linux socket backend and the access gate). The QUIC/TLS/encryption decision is still waiting for the user (before Day 280).

Phase 20 **After Day 267 (2026-11-11), the user's request: scoped test runs stay scoped.** `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` no longer runs the whole suite for a `CMakeLists.txt`, `tests/phases.txt` or `tests/module_map.txt` change that only adds lines (the root `CMakeLists.txt`: only `add_subdirectory` calls and comments), nor for `tools/docgen/CMakeLists.txt`; `cmake/`, `third_party/`, foundation headers and edited or removed lines still do. Day 267's selection drops from `2519 of 6618` to `489 of 6618` (phases 3 4 6 12 20). New `--additive-from-file` option and five cases in `tests/test_phase_script`; `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/guide/building-and-testing.md` updated. The diff reaches back to the start of the phase, so one non-additive edit widens every later day of that phase.

Phase 21 (side) **Day 291 (2026-11-11, the Mac): debugging on macOS, part II.** Breakpad's four macOS private headers are re-vendored from the pinned sentry release (hash checked), and the crash handler runs on macOS. sentry hands `on_crash` only the crashed thread's registers, so `os/macos/debug/crash_hook.cpp` builds the report from them: it finds the crashed thread, reads the reason from that thread's exception state (the context's own exception state is empty: every fault had read as a divide error), and walks the stack safely. Death tests and fault injection are shared with Linux in `os/posix/debug/`. A child watches a pipe from its parent, so it never outlives a parent killed with SIGKILL (tested). The debugger check, post-mortem dumps (brought forward from Day 292) and a Mach-O no-network check are in place. Found and handled: ASan stopped every crashing child on Breakpad's stack copy (suppressed for that one call), and with the handler a crash ends the process with exit code 1, not a signal. Runs (macOS, the script, crash tests included): `ran 1291 of 1291 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; ...)` in both unity modes; the one failure is a TSan timing test that is flaky under load (cause recorded). Code review: 10 findings, 9 fixed (two hangs: a fault inside the report, a pthread lock in the report), 1 in part. Next: Day 292 (dumps, `crash_extract`, scripts).

Phase 20 **Day 268 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): the socket interface, the Linux backend and the access gate.** `net::Socket` (non-blocking, close-on-exec, no `SIGPIPE`; TCP and UDP, IPv4 and IPv6, dual stack, two-step connect, whole datagrams with truncation reported, misuse is `Invalid`) over `engine/net/posix/` and `linux/`; `engine/net/BACKEND.md` with `net_backend_lists`; the gate `net_access_check` (socket calls and headers outside the backends; three allow-list entries for non-socket uses); real-socket tests in `net_tests_sockets` (label `network`, skip with a reason when loopback is unavailable); guide updated. Runs: plain `ran 521 of 6686 tests (phases: 3 4 6 12 20; flavors: plain; display: excluded; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 420 of 6686`, tsan `ran 181 of 6686`, no failures; GCC non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 9 findings, 7 fixed with regression tests (checked by reverting), 2 declined (day file). Open: the Windows PC build, the macOS hooks. **Next: Day 269** (the reactor, awaitables and timers). The QUIC/TLS/encryption decision is still waiting for the user (before Day 280).

**Code style (2026-11-11, the user's request): clang-format tooling.** `.clang-format` (Allman braces, every control-flow body braced by `InsertBraces`, 120 columns, 4 spaces, include order untouched), `.clang-format-ignore` (vendored, generated, fixtures), `scripts/format.sh [--check]`, the pre-commit hook `scripts/git-hooks/pre-commit` with `scripts/install-git-hooks.sh` (once per machine), a "Code style" section in `CLAUDE.md`, `BUILD.md` rows for clang-format and clang-tidy, a guide section, a memory note. Next, in order: the whole-tree formatting pass as its own commit (the Mac machine has nothing unpushed, confirmed by the user), then `.clang-tidy` and `scripts/tidy.sh`, then fixing the existing tidy findings. No code changed in this commit.

**Code style pass (2026-11-11, Linux machine, the user's request): the tree is formatted.** Doc-comment labels became list items (`/// - Fails: ...`; 7,006 lines; docgen reads the form with flush continuation lines and rejects the bare one; `docs/reference` unchanged by the rewrite), then clang-format reformatted all 2,511 tracked C and C++ files outside `third_party/`, generated code and fixtures: Allman braces, braced bodies, 120 columns, wrapped comments (`ReflowComments`), `PenaltyBreakAssignment` so trailing comments wrap under themselves. docgen's scanner continues a `///<` comment on the `///<` lines below it. `scripts/wrap_comments.py` and `scripts/relabel_docs.py` are gone. Run: `ran 6698 of 6698 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: included)`; the one failure is the X11 clipboard window test that failed before the pass too; docs 7 of 7; symbols identical (line numbers moved). Open: the Windows PC and the Mac build the formatted tree (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). **Next: `.clang-tidy` and `scripts/tidy.sh`, then fixing its findings; then Phase 20 Day 269.**

**X11 content scale (2026-11-11, Linux machine, the user's report: X11 demos ignored the screen's HiDPI).** `os::Window::ContentScale()` was 1 on X11 (only Win32 implemented it), so the UI and the overlay drew at 100% on a 2x desktop (`Xft.dpi: 192`; the X server itself says 96 dpi). `X11Window` now reads `Xft.dpi` from the root window's `RESOURCE_MANAGER` (the GTK/Qt/Chromium convention: the last `Xft.dpi` line wins, the tight binding beats `Xft*dpi`, clamped to 1-4, read again on a `PropertyNotify`), `PlatformWindowHost::Open` scales the logical size by it as on Win32 (`X11Window::Create(..., SizeUnits::Logical)`; plain `Create` stays in pixels), and nothing is queued when it changes: callers read it each frame, as the UI adapter does. Tests: `tests/foundation/x11_scale_test.cpp` (the parser, 5 cases) and an X11 window case; `window_backend_test` now expects the scaled size. Runs: plain `ran 2562 of 6703 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1984 of 6703`, tsan `ran 1660 of 6703`, no failures (the whole suite ran because the style pass is inside the phase's diff range); docs 7 of 7. Review: 8 findings; fixed: duplicate-key semantics (last wins, tight beats loose), a property longer than the read cap (whole lines only, 1 MiB), the float-to-u32 overflow of a huge logical size, the `window.h` and guide text (no event on a change), the root event mask note; declined: a second helper for the test's independent property read (it is the oracle), a live property-change test (it would rewrite the user's session property; listed in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Wayland still reports 1 (the compositor scales the window). **Next: `.clang-tidy`, then Phase 20 Day 269.**

**clang-tidy (2026-11-11/12, Linux machine, the user's request): `.clang-tidy`, `scripts/tidy.sh`, and the findings fixed.** A curated set (bugprone, performance, the analyzer's core/NewDelete/DeadStores checks, a few modernize/readability checks; the reasons for every check left out are in `.clang-tidy`; `tests/.clang-tidy` also drops the widening and narrowing checks) runs on `build-tidy` (non-unity, `compile_commands.json`) with `scripts/tidy.sh [--changed] [--summary] [--fix] [FILE...]`; it is not in the commit hook. The first full run found 413 distinct findings in 577 files; the last found **0**. Fixes with a behavior: `ENGINE_ASSERT`/`VERIFY`/`CHECK_SLOW` end the path for the analyzer (`ENGINE_STATIC_ANALYSIS` in `build_defs.h`); `RenderGraph::RollbackTransients` with no pool no longer dereferences it; `SignExtend` of 0 bytes no longer shifts by -1; the X11 drag-and-drop XdndEnter read `types[0]` with no types; `Rng::Below(0)`; `LoadFlags` returns `FT_Int32`; two `Move` calls where a path was copied; ~70 mechanical fixes (`noexcept` on move operations and their definitions, `= default`, `override`, unused `using`s, `nullptr`, popup/atlas/clipboard multiplications widened first, NaN tests through `IsFinite`/`IsNaN`). A fix that broke non-unity builds (a `using` the check could not see) was caught by tidy itself. Suppressions are on their line with a reason (`// NOLINT(check): reason`). Runs: plain `ran 2562 of 6703 tests`, asan `ran 1984 of 6703`, tsan `ran 1660 of 6703` (the two X11 window tests that need an active session failed, as they did before this work); docs 7 of 7. Review: 6 findings; fixed 4 (the assert in the benchmark's timed loop is now `ENGINE_CHECK_SLOW`; the leak suppression is narrowed to one line; a misleading comment; an uninitialized local became a `const` initialized by a conditional), declined 2 (disabling whole check families is documented and deliberate; the doc comments of `death_test.h` and the docs checks are unaffected). **Next: a `tidy_check` ctest gate (a follow-up, not now), then Phase 20 Day 269.**

Phase 20 **Day 269 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): readiness on the job system.** `net::Reactor` (one non-participant thread over `net::Poller`, epoll on Linux, resuming waiting coroutines on participants), `CancelToken`, `Clock`/`ManualClock`, and the asynchronous operations `Receive`/`Send`/`ReceiveFrom`/`SendTo`/`Accept`/`Connect` as `jobs::Task`s (new `engine/jobs/task.h`). The application owns the reactor (declared after the scheduler), not the scheduler; the registrations are level-triggered one-shot. Tested with 1,000 loopback connections x 100 echo messages on 1, 4 and 8 participants, cancel/close/shutdown/race cases, a manual clock, in plain, asan and tsan. Runs: plain `ran 2582 of 6763 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` (the two X11 window tests that need an active session failed, as before), asan `ran 2004 of 6763`, tsan `ran 1680 of 6763`; GCC non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 10 findings, all fixed (day file). Also fixed: `tests/module_map.txt` had no entries for ecs, spatial, storage, text, ui, ui_window, scene, several demos and test directories, which made their changes run the whole suite. Open: the Windows poller (Day 278). **Next: Day 270** (name resolution, listeners, dual stack). The QUIC/TLS/encryption decision is still waiting for the user (before Day 280).

Phase 20 **Day 270 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): names, listeners, dual stack.** `net::Signal`, `net::Resolver` (own thread, bounded queue, abandoned lookups skipped) with `net::SystemResolve` (getaddrinfo, POSIX backend), `net::Resolve`/`ConnectAny`/`ConnectTo` (sequential endpoints, per-attempt timeout), `net::Listener` (dual stack reported honestly, back-off on descriptor exhaustion, cap on unhandled connections), `ErrorCode::NotFound`. Tests: 5,000 accepts, `setrlimit` exhaustion and recovery, resolver flood/abandon/shutdown, IPv6-only vs dual stack. Runs: plain `ran 2598 of 6811 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` (the two X11 window tests that need an active session failed, as before), asan `ran 2020 of 6811`, tsan `ran 1696 of 6811`; GCC non-unity clean; tidy no findings; docs 7 of 7. Review: 10 findings, 9 fixed, 1 declined (day file). Open: the Windows `GetAddrInfoEx` resolver and Winsock (Day 278). **Next: Day 271** (TCP streams). The QUIC/TLS/encryption decision is still waiting for the user (before Day 280).

**Windows PC catch-up (2026-11-12).** After the reformatting, the clang-tidy pass, Phase 20 Days 266-270 and the Mac's Days 284-291: three fixes (the `ComponentDesc` C2797 fix had been undone by the tidy pass, `modernize-use-nullptr` broke an ordering test for the Microsoft library, and the Windows header check now skips `os/posix/`), then four trees clean; `ran 4551 of 4551 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: included) in 413 s`; docs 7 of 7; the three windowed demos 1000 frames with validation on DX12 and Vulkan. Twelve display tests (clipboard, drag and drop, Alt key) failed while the PC was locked and wait for a rerun (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`); otherwise only the known `Throughput` timing flake. The Mac's Day 284-285 checks are done on both machines; Phase 15's Windows close waits only for that rerun. Review of the fixes: no findings. The Winsock backend is Phase 20 Day 278.
Phase 20 **Day 271 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): TCP streams and framing.** `net::FrameWriter`/`FrameReader` (length checked before any payload memory; split-proof), `net::TcpStream` (bounded send queue with backpressure as a value, `Flush`/`SendFrame`/`ReceiveFrame`, graceful `Close` with linger, abortive `Abort`, a `CloseReason` for every end, idle and connect timeouts, keep-alive options), `Socket::SetKeepAliveTiming`/`SetLinger`. Tests: 10,000 frames at every read size, slow-reader backpressure, hostile frames, reset/EOF mid-frame, idle timeout, graceful close, 1,000 streams. Runs: plain `ran 2624 of 6889 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2046 of 6889`, tsan `ran 1722 of 6889` (only the known tsan scene test failed); GCC and Clang non-unity clean; docs 7 of 7. Review: 3 findings, 2 fixed, 1 deferred (per-stream buffers are allocated up front: Day 279). Open: Winsock/Windows poller (Day 278). **Next: Day 272** (NetWriter/NetReader). The QUIC/TLS/encryption decision is still waiting for the user (before Day 280).

Phase 20 **Day 272 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): message codec.** `net::NetWriter`/`NetReader` (bit packing, ranged integers, canonical varints, length-limited strings and arrays, zero-padded alignment), deterministic quantization (floats, Vec2/3/4, smallest-three quaternions, angles) with documented error bounds, integer and quantized deltas; every failure is a value and changes nothing. Tests: 1,000,000 mixed sequences, every truncation, golden bytes, dense sweeps. Runs: plain `ran 2646 of 6970 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2068 of 6970`, tsan `ran 1744 of 6970` (only the known tsan scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity and an optimized tree pass the codec tests; docs 7 of 7. Review: 4 findings, 2 fixed, 2 declined. Open: the Windows PC runs the golden bytes (Day 278).
Phase 20 **Day 273 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): transport interface, simulated network and transport, conformance suite.** `net::Transport` (polled events, four reliability kinds on channels, backpressure as a value), `net::SimNetwork` (deterministic, per-direction impairments, trace) and `net::SimulatedTransport` (own reliability protocol); `tests/net/transport_conformance.h` runs 13 checks on a clean and a hostile link; each impairment measured over 1,000,000 packets. Runs: plain `ran 2680 of 7072 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2102 of 7072`, tsan `ran 1778 of 7072` (only the known tsan scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity and an optimized tree pass; docs 7 of 7. Review: 9 findings, all fixed with 4 regression tests. Open: the Windows PC runs it (Day 278). **Next: Day 274 (UDP transport), after the QUIC/TLS decision is made by the user before Day 280.**
Phase 20 **Day 274 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): UDP transport I, packet layer and handshake.** `net::UdpTransport` over a `net::DatagramPort` (real socket or simulated network): stateless cookie handshake (SipHash-2-4, full-size requests only, no amplification), connection table with caps, 16-bit sequences with ack bitfield across wraparound, Disconnect/Reset, keep-alive; unreliable messages only until Day 275. Tests: handshake under 0-60% loss, spoofed floods, replays, capacity, wraparound, loopback sockets. Runs: plain `ran 2695 of 7117 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2117 of 7117`, tsan `ran 1793 of 7117` (only the known tsan scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity, optimized pass; docs 7 of 7. Review: 5 findings, 4 fixed, 1 declined (filed). Open: the Windows PC (Day 278).

Phase 20 **Day 275 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): UDP transport II, channels, reliability and fragmentation.** All four reliability kinds on `net::UdpTransport`: several messages per packet, channel priority, resend from acknowledgements and time-outs, ordered delivery with a bounded reorder buffer, fragmentation with exact validation and per-connection reassembly budgets. The conformance suite passes at 0, 5, 30 and 60% loss with reordering and duplication; a 1 MiB message arrives intact under each; hostile fragment streams stay within budget. Runs: plain `ran 2757 of 7303 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 1935 of 6541`, tsan `ran 1617 of 6541` (only the known tsan scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity, optimized pass; docs 7 of 7. Review: 8 findings, 6 fixed, 2 declined (one filed). Open: the Windows PC (Day 278).

Phase 20 **Day 276 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): UDP transport III, round trip, congestion control, giving up.** NewReno-style window with pacing and an application cap, time-outs from the smoothed round trip, oldest-unreliable-first discard when the queue is full, `Disconnect` during the handshake, richer statistics. Measured on the simulated network: about 98% of a 1 Mbit/s / 40 ms cap, two connections within a factor of two, blackouts survived or timed out, RTT within 10%. Runs: plain `ran 2768 of 7336 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2190 of 7336` (one flaky benchmark, passed alone), tsan `ran 1866 of 7336` (only the known scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity, optimized pass; docs 7 of 7. Review: 7 findings, 5 fixed, 2 declined. Open: the Windows PC (Day 278).
Phase 20 **Day 277 (2026-11-12, Linux machine): the TCP transport.** `net::TcpTransport` over a `StreamProvider` (real sockets and a simulated stream), Hello/Data/Close/Ping/Pong frames, unreliable kinds sent reliable ordered, bounded queues, close linger. Conformance on the simulated stream (whole and one-byte reads), hostile streams, real loopback (`network`), and one scripted conversation compared across simulated, UDP and TCP. Not written: the 1,000-mixed-connections tsan test. Runs: plain `ran 2804 of 7444 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` (known flaky benchmark failed, passed alone), asan `ran 2226 of 7444`, tsan `ran 1902 of 7444` (only the known scene test failed); GCC, Clang non-unity, optimized pass; docs 7 of 7. Review: 5 findings, 4 fixed, 1 declined. Open: the Windows PC. **Next: Day 278 (Windows socket backend).**
Phase 21 (side) **Day 292 (2026-11-12, the Mac): debugging on macOS, part III; the debugging backend is complete.** A crash's minidump is read and turned into a trace on macOS: `crash_extract` now reads Breakpad's macOS dumps (their module and thread lists are padded after the count: every module had read 4 bytes off), and its new `--lldb` mode completes the register flags Breakpad leaves out (without it `lldb` showed every thread at pc 0). **Plan amended:** the macOS resolver is `lldb`, run by `crash-report.sh`, as on Linux, not an in-process one. `store-symbols.sh`, `split-debug.sh`, `crash-report.sh`, `find-core.sh` and `open-core.sh` have macOS branches (dSYM store `<name>/<UUID>/<name>.dSYM`, one per architecture; `lldb`; `kern.corefile`; bash 3.2; paths with spaces), tested by the new `tests/debug/macos/scripts_macos_test.cpp`: a stripped program gets file and line back through the store and not without it, cross-checked with `atos`. `/cores` is root's here, so the core test uses a core `lldb` saves. Also from the pull: the main line's clang-tidy pass broke the Mac build (`< nullptr` on an ordering in `tests/types/int128_test.cpp`; the main line fixed it the same day with the named orderings, kept on the rebase), and its Phase 20 gate needed a macOS flavor line. **The Mac has no usable `clang-format`** (Apple's 17 differs from 23.1.4 and ignores `.clang-format-ignore`; `BUILD.md`); the day's files were formatted one by one and checked. Runs (macOS, the script, crash tests included): `ran 1363 of 1363 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: included)`, the three failures timing tests flaky under load that pass alone. Code review: 9 findings, 7 fixed, 2 in part (a dump cut short gave no trace; the store deleted a good bundle before copying). Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared scripts and `crash_extract` (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 293 (settings store, docgen on the Mac).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 293 (2026-11-12, the Mac): settings on macOS; the foundation port is done.** Every layer of the foundation (base, file, debugging, settings) and the `foundation` aggregate build on macOS in three flavors, and `engine/jobs` links the aggregate. `UserDefaultsConfigStore` (`settings/macos/`, through `CFPreferences`) passes the store conformance suite: one defaults suite per store, values as their canonical text (as the registry store, so enums round-trip), each write durable at once. **Decided, with measurements:** the user's settings stay the file, as on Windows; the defaults store is what a program adds when it wants `defaults` (0.17 ms per write against 39 ms for a durable file save); managed preferences are the policy layer. macOS does not announce another process's writes (checked), so the store watches the domain itself every 250 ms, never reporting its own writes. Declined: a `System` layer from bundle defaults (they are the program's own shipped defaults). docgen builds on the Mac and regenerates `docs/reference/` byte-identical to Linux's; `ctest -L docs` passes (the samples wait for Day 311). Runs (macOS, the script, crash tests included): `ran 2108 of 2108 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: included)` in both unity modes, the only failures the two timing tests flaky under load. Code review: 10 findings, 8 fixed, 1 declined (sharing the watch code with the Windows store), 1 the STATUS update. Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared changes (Windows' job system now links the settings layer; `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 294 (the CPU modules: text, UI, ECS, spatial). **Later the same day: the Mac has `clang-format` 23.1.5** (MacPorts `clang-23`, linked as `clang-format`; `BUILD.md`): `scripts/format.sh --check` passes over the whole tree (2,656 files) after one Day 293 comment line was rewrapped in its own commit. The pre-commit hook is not installed on the Mac yet (`scripts/install-git-hooks.sh`, the user's to run).

Phase 21 (side) **plan change (2026-11-12, the Mac, the user's decision): a networking day.** The main line's Phase 19 and 20 modules arrived after Phase 21 was planned. `engine/database` (with SQLite) and `engine/storage` join Day 294 (window-free, file access only through the file layer). `engine/net` needs platform code on macOS (socket hooks, a `kqueue` poller beside Linux's `epoll`), so it gets a day of its own, the new **Day 310**, after Phase 20 closes on the main line (Day 281). Phase 21 is now **Days 282-312** (the demos day is 327, the closing day 328; renumbered in the same commit, earlier log entries keep their numbers). Next: Day 294.

Phase 21 (side) **Day 294 (2026-11-12, the Mac): text, UI (CPU side), ECS, spatial, database and storage on macOS.** All six build at the first attempt (FreeType and SQLite included, no warning, no code change for Apple clang or libc++) and pass their suites in plain, asan and tsan, in both unity modes; the text atlas matches Linux's goldens byte for byte. The window-bound UI tests wait for Day 295, networking for the new Day 310. Benchmarks on the 4-core i5 (Release, one run each): ECS iteration at 100k 19.8 times the baseline, 82 percent parallel efficiency at 4 workers, every pass condition met; 100,000 moving agents 171 ms a frame in a tree, 34 ms in a grid (`docs/guide/ecs.md`, `spatial.md`). Runs (macOS, the script): `ran 4114 of 4493 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, the failures the flaky throughput test and a stale `symbols.txt` (Day 293's formatting commit; regenerated). Code review: 8 findings, all fixed or answered, none a run-time bug. Next: Day 295 (the Cocoa window).

**Windows PC catch-up (2026-11-12, after Phase 20 Day 276 and the Mac's Day 294).** Nothing to fix: the four trees (MSVC and clang-cl, unity and non-unity) built clean; `ran 4863 of 4863 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: included) in 388 s` in `build-msvc`; 2673 of 2673 tests in `build-clang-cl` (plain); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7 with `docs/reference/` regenerated unchanged; `rhi_demo`, `renderer_demo` and `ui_demo` 1000 frames with validation on DX12 and Vulkan. The only failures were the twelve locked-session display tests (six in `build-clang-cl`), still waiting for a rerun on an unlocked desktop, and the known `Throughput` timing flake once in `build-clang-cl`. The Mac's Days 286-290 are done on both machines; Days 291-294 now wait for the Linux machine only. Phase 20's platform-neutral half (Days 271-276: framing, codec, simulated and UDP transport) is built and tested on Windows; the sockets wait for the Winsock backend, Day 278. **Then Day 277 (the TCP transport):** the four trees built clean again, `ran 4656 of 4933 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 349 s` (CMake lines edited, so everything but the crash tests) with only the twelve locked-session tests and the known `Skewed load` asan flake (5 of 5 on rerun), and `net_tests` passes its 204 cases in all four trees (`sim_stream`, `tcp_transport`, the cross-transport comparison; the loopback test waits for Day 278).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 295 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the Cocoa window.** `engine/platform` builds on macOS: `os::cocoa::CocoaWindow` (an `NSWindow` with a `CAMetalLayer`-backed view, pumped by the program's own loop), the window host (`WindowBackend::Cocoa`, `platform.window_backend` gains `cocoa`) and the entry point, which sets up a regular app with a Quit menu. Windows are refused off the main thread (the test found the check came too late; fixed and shown failing first); sizes are points in, pixels out at the backing scale; the close button and Cmd-Q are close requests the program answers, and a logout or shutdown waits for the program instead of being cancelled; native fullscreen. `hello_window` builds and runs. Spike C's rerun, unlocked, confirmed activation from a terminal (104 ms) and display-paced frames (59 Hz); no `Info.plist` is needed. Runs (macOS): the `display` group 44 of 44 while unlocked, no skips, and `ran 4114 of 4525 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: excluded; crash tests: excluded)`, the failure the flaky throughput test. Not done by hand: closing `hello_window` from the keyboard (the VNC link was too poor; the tests drive both paths through AppKit). Code review: 10 findings, all fixed (one by another route). Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared changes, including the demos' goldens (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 296 (keyboard and mouse input).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 296 (2026-11-12, the Mac): keyboard, text, pointer and wheel on macOS.** The Cocoa window fills `InputState` and the event stream.
Keys are layout-aware (`os::cocoa::KeyFromCocoa`, `platform/macos/cocoa_keys.h`, checked for every `Key` against the SDK's `kVK_` codes). Option is Alt and Command is
Super; Option types (macOS's third level) and Control and Command do not. Text comes through `interpretKeyEvents:` and a minimal `NSTextInputClient`, so dead keys work.
The pointer is in pixels from the top left, with moves from an always-active tracking area. A trackpad scroll is 10 points to the notch. A focus loss releases every
held key before `FocusLost`. Two fixes: a key-up with Command held is given to its window (AppKit keeps it otherwise; a test shows it), and press-and-hold is off for the
program (found by hand: held letters repeated without text). `input_probe` builds on the Mac and was checked by hand on the Spanish ISO layout: dead accents, Option
text, repeat, Cmd-Tab with a key held, clicks, scrolling, moves while unfocused. Not done by hand: the US layout, a drag past the edge, the number pad. Runs (macOS):
`ran 4219 of 4598 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, with the flaky throughput test failing; the screen locked partway, and
the `display` group rerun unlocked passed 105 of 105. Code review: 8 findings, all fixed (two with regression tests shown failing first: two keys of one name, and moves
outside the window). Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the shared files (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 297 (cursor, clipboard, drag and drop).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 297 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the cursor, the pointer lock, the clipboard, and drag and drop on macOS.** The window layer's
contract is complete on the Mac (`platform/macos/cocoa_cursor.mm`, `cocoa_clipboard.mm`, `cocoa_dragdrop.mm`). Cursors: the ten shapes as `NSCursor`s,
with the diagonals from macOS 15 and the arrow before that, and the open hand for `ResizeAll`. Custom images come at their scale. `[NSCursor hide]` is kept
to one per window on every path. The lock warps the cursor to the centre and detaches it; it needs no Accessibility permission. The clipboard is `NSPasteboard`, with
file lists through the shared `ParseUriList` rules and the system keeping the data after close. Drag and drop works both ways, the data copied at the drop.
Bugs found by the tests: `café` decomposed in file URLs, and typed text with a lone surrogate vanished. Found by hand: the lock's first motion
carried the warp's 200-point jump, and a drag out left the button down. All fixed, each with a test. Hand check with Finder and TextEdit: copy and paste both ways, drops from
both, a text drag out taken by TextEdit, shapes, image and lock. Runs (macOS): `ran 4264 of 4643 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash
tests: excluded)`, failing the flaky throughput test and one test under a title renamed mid-run. After the fixes the `display` group passed 159 of 159. Code review: 10 findings,
8 fixed and 2 in part. Not done by hand: a file dragged to Finder, `CancelDrag` mid-drag, the drop timeout, macOS 13/14 (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 298
(the RHI, the renderer and the demos on the null backend).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 298 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the RHI, the renderer, the scene bridge, the UI window adapter and the demos on macOS, on the null
backend.** Every module above the platform now builds on the Mac, in both unity modes. `rhi_vulkan` is not built there (decision 8), and the shared tests keep their Vulkan
cases behind `ENGINE_RHI_HAVE_VULKAN`, defined by `rhi_vulkan` (the list is in the port spec). `rhi::Backend::Metal` is new: `Auto` moves from 3 to 4, and `metal` is "not built yet" until
Day 300. An unregistered backend a platform does not build is refused as "not built for <platform>". On macOS the `renderer.backend` setting's `auto` is null while no GPU
backend is built (`renderer::BackendForSetting`). `rhi::PickAutoBackend` itself never picks null; tried there first, the fallback handed a GPU test a null device. The three
demos run their scripted resets and recoveries in a Cocoa window on null, and their goldens pass. GPU tests skip ("no GPU backend"). The demos' tests got a private `HOME`
(review: they were reading the developer's profile). Runs (macOS): `ran 5834 of 6214 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`,
failing the flaky throughput test and two stale sanitizer binaries, both passing once rebuilt. Code review: 9 findings, 8 fixed, 1 in part (the benchmarks keep failing on macOS until Metal, on
purpose). Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the many shared changes (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 299 (the Metal shader path).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 299 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the Metal shader path. Tests deferred to the end of Day 309 (the user's choice for Days 299-309:
build, review, commit and push each day; test at the end).** Every shader and permutation key now has a committed `.metallib.inc`: an AIR-only,
executable library, its entry name (`main_0`) and the hash of the SPIR-V beside it. `tools/metal_mint` mints them from `slangc -target metal`
through the driver's compiler (the interim, undocumented `serializeToURL:` route of Day 282, guarded and checked before writing). The 120 outputs
were byte-identical on a second mint. Shared changes:
- **The reflection** (format 3) records each binding's Metal slot on every platform; slangc's Metal build needs no Mac.
- **`ShaderFormat::Msl` is now `MetalLib`**, and a blob carries its entry name.
- **`register()` is emitted for D3D12 only** (`ENGINE_REGISTER`). The SPIR-V and reflection were re-minted byte-identical.
- **The Metal build shares D3D12's** clip-space flip and object index stream.
- **`fill.comp` and `saxpy.comp`** take their element count as a push constant: new SPIR-V, and DXIL stale until the PC re-mints it.
- **Off a Mac,** a placeholder `.metallib.inc` keeps every build compiling.

Code review: 10 findings, all fixed. The worst were a new test that read DXIL as Metal, and a missing placeholder that would have broken
Linux and Windows on the next new shader. A formatter call fed every changed file rewrote the day's text files as C++; they were restored and the
edits replayed. Waiting: the PC's DXIL re-mint, and the Linux and Windows checks (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 300 (the Metal device).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 300 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the Metal device. Tests deferred to Day 309.** `rhi_metal` (`engine/rhi/metal/`, Objective-C++
with ARC) is built and registered on macOS. It has:
- **Adapters:** every `MTLDevice`, with the registry ID as the id.
- **The device:** one queue, and an `MTLSharedEvent` timeline as the fence.
- **Waits** in 100 ms slices that end on a lost device.
- **Frames in flight,** as on D3D12.
- **The capabilities and formats** of the device's family.
- **Command-buffer errors and loss,** reported as on the other backends.

**Plan change:** Metal is registered at auto rank 0, as D3D12 was before it passed the suites, so it is used only when asked for by name
(`--rhi metal`). The Day 298 fallback ("auto is null on macOS") stays until Day 309, when Metal can draw. Validation is advisory on Metal
(an environment setting read at process start). The macOS RHI ctests now run with Metal's API and shader validation, and the macOS `[gpu]` tests
run under TSan too. The planted program `metal_validation_planted` shows that a validation error stops a run.

Code review: 10 findings, all fixed. The worst were the Catch2 discovery patch flattening a second environment variable, which had stripped every
macOS RHI test of its phase label, and a use after free in the completion handlers (they now hold shared state, not the device). Built in both
unity modes; runs deferred to Day 309. Waiting: the Linux and Windows runs of the Catch2 discovery patch (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 301
(Metal buffers and textures).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 301 (2026-11-12, the Mac): Metal buffers and textures. Tests deferred to Day 309.** Buffers and textures with deferred
destruction, counts and the injected allocation failure. GPU-only memory and textures are `Private`, readback memory and the staging ring `Shared`,
and upload memory `Shared` on unified memory or `Managed` otherwise. A test hook forces `Managed` here, its only coverage on this unified Mac.
Uploads go through the staging ring in bands and readbacks through a temporary buffer; buffer copies use the 4-byte-aligned window Metal needs.
Shared change: `rhi::MipExtent` in `rhi/format.h` replaces five private copies. Code review: 10 findings, all fixed. The worst were unaligned
buffer copies (invalid on macOS) and a stale persistent mapping of `Managed` readback memory. Built in both unity modes; runs deferred to Day 309.
Waiting: the Linux and Windows builds of the `MipExtent` change (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 302 (samplers and bind groups).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 302 (2026-11-12, the Mac): Metal bind groups, samplers and slots. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **Slot tables (plan amended):** Slang picks each Metal library's slots itself, so every minted library now carries a slot table,
  `rhi::ShaderSlotTable` (set, binding, slot, type and count, plus the push-constant slot). The blob carries it into `ShaderDesc`.
  The 29 libraries were re-minted (the library bytes unchanged, byte-identical twice) and the `.dxil.inc` files edited to pass the tables.
- **Bindings:** samplers shared by description, layouts, and groups held by handle; transient groups end with their frame.
- **Slot maps:** `MapStageSlots` matches a stage's table against a pipeline's layouts; every mismatch is refused with its reason.
- **Binder:** it skips unchanged slots, starts empty on each new encoder, and writes push constants. Decision 9 (direct binding) stands
  until the Day 309 measurement.

Code review: 10 findings, 9 fixed, 1 in part (sampler sharing kept as on D3D12). The worst was the binder choosing an argument table by
which object was present, which could write past its arrays. Built in both unity modes; runs deferred to Day 309. Waiting: the PC's
re-mint must reproduce the `.dxil.inc` edit (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). Next: Day 303 (shaders and pipelines).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 303 (2026-11-12, the Mac): Metal shaders, pipelines and the pipeline cache. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **Shaders:** made from the minted libraries.
- **Render pipelines:** vertex buffers at the top of the buffer table, blending, depth formats; the encoder state is kept for the command
  lists.
- **Compute pipelines:** each keeps the threadgroup size its shader declares, now minted in the slot table (the libraries were re-minted
  byte-identical).
- **Pipeline cache:** an `MTLBinaryArchive` behind a header naming the adapter and macOS build. A miss compiles once into the archive; the
  archive is written atomically, and a damaged or foreign file is discarded.

Code review: 10 findings, 8 fixed, 2 declined (sharing D3D12's cache header code; keeping each shader's bytes). The worst were two devices
sharing the archive's files and archive lookups beside unlocked adds. Built in both unity modes; runs and the `renderer_demo` start-up
measurement deferred to Day 309. Next: Day 304 (command lists).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 304 (2026-11-12, the Mac): Metal command lists. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **Recording:** each list records into its own retained command buffer, so lists can be recorded on several jobs. `Submit` commits them in
  the order given, then signals the timeline once.
- **Encoders:** a render encoder per pass; blit and compute encoders open as needed. The pipeline, groups and push constants are applied
  again on each new encoder.
- **Commands:**
  - barriers become a memory barrier inside a compute encoder, and nothing elsewhere;
  - draws use base instance and base vertex, and strips always restart;
  - dispatches use the minted threadgroup;
  - mips come from a blit, and debug labels survive encoder splits.

Code review: 10 findings, 9 fixed, 1 left for the Day 309 run (UInt16 index offsets). The worst were aborts after a recording failure and a
pass dropping a bound compute pipeline. Built in both unity modes; runs deferred to Day 309, after the user says go. Next: Day 305 (the
swapchain).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 305 (2026-11-12, the Mac): the Metal swapchain. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **The layer:** the Cocoa window's `CAMetalLayer`.
- **Acquire:** `nextDrawable`, within the layer's one-second timeout; a timeout or a minimized window counts as `Minimized`.
- **Present:** `presentDrawable:` after the frame's lists, and the drawable is never kept.
- **Present modes:** `Fifo`, `Immediate`, and `Mailbox` falling back to `Fifo`.
- **Formats and HDR:** `bgra8_srgb` and `bgra8_unorm`; HDR only on an EDR screen.
- **Resizes and surfaces:** resizes follow the window, and `RecreateSurface` re-binds to the window's layer.

Four amendments to the plan are recorded in the day file. Code review: 10 findings, all fixed. The worst was the drawable size no longer
following the window after an explicit `Resize`. Built in both unity modes; runs deferred to Day 309 (with the fullscreen and Retina hand
checks). Next: Day 306 (golden images).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 306 (2026-11-13, the Mac): Metal golden images. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **The suites:** the Phase 4 golden suites already run once per GPU backend, so on macOS they run through Metal unchanged. Their numbers
  are taken in the Day 309 run, and no tolerance was touched.
- **Swapchain readback:** an image reads back what the same drawing gives offscreen. On Metal it can be read only until `Present`, and
  the contract now says so.
- **Vulkan-only tests:** they skip on macOS, even when a GPU is required.

Code review: 8 findings, 7 fixed, 1 declined (a cross-thread readback, which the swapchain contract excludes). Built in both unity modes;
runs deferred to Day 309. Next: Day 307 (the renderer on Metal).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 307 (2026-11-13, the Mac): the renderer on Metal. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **No renderer changes:** the renderer needs nothing of its own, and its GPU suites run once per backend, so through Metal on macOS.
- **Validation:** every Metal run (RHI tests, renderer tests, demos) now has Metal's validation environment, and the renderer's GPU tests run
  under TSan on macOS.
- **`renderer_demo`:** its macOS ctests run on Metal, plus three named Metal tests (screenshot, loss trace, out of memory).
- **Graph test:** a new test checks a graph's writing and sampling passes are separate render encoders.

Code review: 8 findings, 5 fixed, 1 in part, 2 declined. The worst was the new Metal runs having validation off. Built in both unity modes;
runs, the `renderer_demo.ppm` comparison, the benchmark baselines and the live keys deferred to Day 309. Next: Day 308 (text, UI and scene
on Metal).

Phase 21 (side) **Day 308 (2026-11-13, the Mac): text, UI and the scene bridge on Metal. Tests deferred to Day 309.**
- **Inventory:** 26 GPU test files run once per backend, so through Metal on macOS: text, UI, `ui_demo`, the scene bridge and the
  renderer's. The Vulkan-only and D3D12-only ones are listed in the day file.
- **Demos:** `ui_demo`'s macOS scripted run uses Metal, and there are four new named Metal ctests (`metal_ui_demo_screenshot`,
  `rhi_demo`'s loss, out-of-memory and listing). The scripted runs forbid Metal validation output.
- **Guides:** `text.md` and `ui.md` name Metal.

Code review: 9 findings, all fixed. The worst was the UI Metal screenshot carrying phase 15, so a phase-26 run would have left it out.
Built in both unity modes; runs and the `ui_demo` hand checks deferred to Day 309. Next: Day 309 (lifecycle, loss, resets; Auto picks
Metal), then the user is asked before the tests run.

Phase 21 (side) **Day 309 (2026-11-13, the Mac): the Metal lifecycle, device loss and resets; `Auto` picks Metal on macOS. Days 299-309
are run: the Metal backend passes the RHI, renderer, text, UI and scene suites with Metal's validation in three flavors.**
- **`Auto`:** it picks Metal on macOS, and the Day 298 null fallback is gone.
- **Device loss:** a device observer marks the device lost when the GPU is removed. A GPU whose removal was requested is left out of the
  next pick.
- **Conformance:** a Metal case in the lifecycle conformance suite; `metal_lifecycle_test` (100 resets with the archive, a stale handle, a
  loss after real work).
- **Validation:** a change that differs from the process says it takes effect at the next start.

Code review: 10 findings, 9 fixed, 1 declined.

**The run of Days 299-309** (after the user said go; Day 309 file): `ran 6110 of 6490 tests (phases: all; flavors: all; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 1479 s`; left only the known stale DXIL test (the PC re-mints `fill.comp`,
`saxpy.comp` and `material_test.frag`) and the known flaky `Throughput scales with participant count` (phase 2, plain; failed in three of the four three-flavor runs, passed 3 of 3 reruns each time). The first plain run failed 62 of 2376; the fixes: a nil sampler label (fatal under
validation), an unused vertex layout, the archive off under shader validation (Metal stops inside it), autorelease pools (a leak of about
37 MiB per 100 resets; ctest `metal_autorelease_pools`), a capture-free timeline block (TSan), `material_test.frag`'s `float3` layout (and
`shader_mint` refusing a Metal offset mismatch), the cache directory created before serializing, a fresh archive when Metal will not
re-serialize, the command shortcuts on Command (`os::kModShortcut`, found by the user), and test, gate and demo-test fixes. Measured: goldens
within tolerance (none changed), a reset 43 ms mean, `rhi_bench` 0.64-0.89 us per draw, `renderer_bench` 1.4-1.5 ms for 1000 objects. Hand
checks by the user: `renderer_demo` F1-F10 (F11 is macOS's) and `ui_demo` input, clipboard, Finder drop and fullscreen. Review of the run's
fixes: 10 findings, 8 fixed, 1 in part, 1 declined. Both unity modes clean; docs 6 of 6. Waiting: the Linux and PC runs of the shared files
(`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). **Next: Day 310.**

Phase 21 (side) **Day 310 (2026-11-13, the Mac): networking on macOS.** `engine/net/macos/`: the socket hooks (flags set right after
`socket`/`accept`, the close-on-exec window documented; `SO_NOSIGPIPE`; a connection reset before accept skipped; `EPROTOTYPE` from a send is Closed)
and a kqueue poller (one-shot read and write filters, an `EVFILT_USER` wake). Shared POSIX changes: datagrams through `recvmsg`'s `MSG_TRUNC` flag
(cut datagrams were not seen on macOS), `ENOTSUP`, and `FinishConnect` reading the pending error again (a refusal misread under load). macOS drops a
connection whose `accept` runs out of descriptors, and its loopback frees room after `send` returns: the tests account for both. `engine/net`,
`tests/net`, `net_probe` and `net_demo` build and pass on macOS in three flavors and both unity modes. Phase 20 **Day 278 (2026-11-12, Windows PC): the Windows socket backend.** Winsock 2 in `engine/net/windows/` (`winsock.h` the one include of the Winsock headers; the error tables; sockets not inherited and non-blocking from creation, dual stack and exclusive ports as the interface's defaults, `SIO_UDP_CONNRESET` off; `GetAddrInfoW` in UTF-16). The poller is `select`, not the planned `WSAPoll`: measured, `WSAPoll` reports a half-closed socket whatever was asked for, which would make the reactor spin; the spec and `BACKEND.md` say so. A refused loopback connect is immediate (SYN retransmission off for loopback; Windows otherwise retries for two seconds). The whole of `tests/net` runs on Windows: four trees clean, `net_tests` and `net_tests_sockets` pass in all of them, plain and asan; the five-thousand-connection listener test hung under load (ephemeral ports exhausted, the accept loop waiting for ever) and now times out and closes server-first. Run: `ran 4806 of 5083 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 376 s`; docs 7 of 7. Review: 4 findings, 2 fixed (a socket closed while the reactor waited broke the reactor for good: `WSAENOTSOCK` from `select`, regression test `poller_test.cpp`; stale comments), 2 declined with measurements (full-backlog refusals, the result mapping cost: `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`). The shared changes wait for the Linux machine (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

**Phase 22 Day 329 done (2026-11-14, Linux): GPU-backed resources, the level spawner and surviving a device reset.** Built on the user's rule of the day (classes share a `SharedPtr` to a small class that holds the data, never a raw pointer into each other's storage): `SharedBytes` and `asset::BlobBuffer` (a blob's pooled buffer, shared by the manager's slot, the loaded object and the GPU objects; the last holder returns it; counted as resident until then), `renderer::MeshGeometry` (sections by offset) with `GpuMesh::CreateFromGeometry`, `GpuTexture::CreateFromSharedLevels`, the GPU loaders (`GpuMeshAsset`, `GpuTextureAsset`, `GpuMaterialAsset` over shared `GpuMesh`, `GpuTexture`, `GpuMaterial`), the fallbacks (unit cube, checker, magenta material, behind `assets.allow_fallbacks`), `scene::SpawnLevel`/`DestroyLevel` (the new edge `scene -> asset`; included levels once; the level holds a share of its materials), `animation::ManagerResolver`. A spawned level renders pixel-identical to the hand-built scene of the same loaded resources on Vulkan; a null-device reset with every kind of object and a skinned mesh keeps the counts; a mesh outlives the manager's resource and survives a reset. Runs: plain `ran 3115 of 8360 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2530 of 8360`, tsan `ran 2203 of 8360`, no failures; Clang and GCC (non-unity) clean; tidy no findings; docs 7 of 7. Review: 9 findings, 8 fixed, 1 declined (day file). The audit the user asked for found older raw-pointer sharing (list in `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`); **Next: the refactor the user will plan, then Day 330 (asynchronous loading).**

**Shared-ownership gate and alias convention (2026-11-14, Linux, the user's request after the Day 329 audit).** New rule in `CLAUDE.md` ("Shared representations"): classes that need the same data share a `SharedPtr` to a small class that holds it; no raw pointer, reference or span into another class's storage; no owned-and-shared twin members; every `SharedPtr<Type>` is spelled by its alias (`GpuMeshPtr`, `GpuTextureConstPtr`, `SharedBytesPtr`, `BlobBufferPtr`, `GpuMaterialPtr`, ...), defined beside the class. The gate `shared_unit_check` (`tools/shared_unit_check`: `units.txt`, `check.cmake`, fixtures and eight ctests; label phase 22) fails on a raw-pointer or reference member of a shared unit, on a `ByteSpan` member in the blob and renderer code, on a spelled-out `SharedPtr<Unit>` and on a missing alias; its `allowlist.txt` lists today's 20 debt files with their reasons (a stale entry fails). Day 329's new code uses the aliases. The refactor that removes the debts (including `scene::MeshAsset`'s owned-and-shared members) is not planned yet. Runs: plain `ran 3123 of 8368 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)`, asan `ran 2530 of 8368`, tsan `ran 2203 of 8368`, no failures; docs 7 of 7.
**Borrowing modes removed (2026-11-14, Linux, outside the plan, the user's request after the audit).** The classes that had a mode where they read another instance's memory instead of their own: `GpuMesh` has one source (the geometry it keeps: `MakeRecreatable(resources, const MeshData&, name)` copies it into a shared region, `CreateFromGeometry` shares the bytes it is given; `Source`, `AdoptSource` and the loader source are gone); `scene::MeshAsset` is gone (`MeshRenderer::mesh` is a `GpuMeshPtr`; `RenderSync` keeps a share per scene object); `GpuTexture::CreateFromLevels` and `TextureLevelRef` are gone (`CreateFromSharedLevels` only); `HostMemoryBackend` always copies (`AddBorrowed`, `ReplaceBorrowed` and `ReleaseBytes` are gone); `MaterialSystem` shares the textures and samplers it follows (`SetTexture`/`SetSampler` take `GpuTextureConstPtr`/`GpuSamplerConstPtr`; the material asset no longer keeps a duplicate list); `LoadContext` has no span beside its shared buffer. `MeshGeometry::Validate` accepts point lists (as `MeshData` does), with a regression test. Not done: the window `InputState*` of `Win32Window` and `CocoaWindow` (code that cannot be built on this machine), and the second group of the audit (views over spans, `RenderObject::mesh`, `GpuTextureImage`, the backend wiring). The allowlist lost three entries (`material.cpp`, `mesh.h`, `resource_manager.h`, `memory_backend_test.cpp`). Post-implementation code review (`/code-review high` over the tree): 8 findings, 5 fixed (`~MaterialSystem` lets go of its shares; the stale guide text and tracking files; a regression test that a material keeps a followed texture alive and `DestroyMaterial` releases it; a dead comment; `OwnedBytes` simplified), 3 declined or documented: the demo's textures live as long as the materials that bind them (the comment says so), `MakeRecreatable` copies the geometry once (the engine's `Vector` aborts on memory exhaustion everywhere), and point lists are valid geometry because `MeshData` and `Create` already accept them. Runs: plain `ran 3121 of 8362`, asan `ran 2528`, tsan `ran 2201`, no failures (the known X11 and benchmark flakes passed on rerun); Clang non-unity and GCC non-unity build clean; GCC unity fails only in `engine/net` (KNOWN-ISSUES); clang-tidy over the 9 changed sources is clean; docs 7 of 7.

**Out of plan (2026-11-14, the user's request): programs read their command line through `os::CommandLine`.** About twenty programs parsed `argv` by hand. `os::CommandLine` now takes declared options (`os::CommandLineOption`: a name, whether it takes a value, whether it repeats; one-dash names such as `-I`): `--name value`, `--name=value`, bare flags, positional arguments, `--` to end the flags, an unknown option or a missing value reported by `Ok()` and `Error()`, `OptionCount`/`OptionAt` for a repeated option and `OptionOr`. The gate `cmdline_access_check` (`tools/cmdline_access_check`, ctest `cmdline_access_check`, planted-violation self-tests, allow list with reasons) fails on `argv[i]` in `engine/`, `game/`, `tools/` and `tests/`; the six `config_access_check` entries of the demos that no longer hold a `"--flag"` string were removed.
